# ===================================================================
#  Subtitle Translator - glossary (sözlük)
# ===================================================================
#
# This file tells the program how to handle special words: names,
# methods, tests and professional terms of child development, special
# education and therapy. The terms come from DİLGEM's own training
# materials and from the Turkish professional literature (DSM-5 Türkçe,
# DergiPark articles, MEB Özel Eğitim Hizmetleri Yönetmeliği, Turkish
# test adaptations); see docs/GLOSSARY.md for sources and choices.
#
# Edit it with Notepad and save it (keep the UTF-8 encoding).
# Changes are used the next time you start a video.
#
# Lines starting with # are comments: the program ignores them.
# Upper/lower case does not matter when the program LOOKS for a word
# ("dilgem", "Dilgem" and "DİLGEM" are the same), except for short
# abbreviations of up to 4 capital letters (ABA, CARS, DİB): those only
# match in capitals, so everyday words like "aba" or "cars" are left alone.
# Only whole words are matched ("trainee" does not match inside "trainees").
#
# -------------------------------------------------------------------
# 1) WORDS TO KEEP EXACTLY AS WRITTEN
# -------------------------------------------------------------------
# Names of people, institutions, methods, tests... One per line, at the
# top, before any [...] section. They are never translated, always
# written exactly like this (e.g. "Dilgem'in" becomes "DİLGEM'in").
# Do NOT put normal Turkish or English words here: they would never be
# translated. Put those in section 4.

# --- DİLGEM

# -------------------------------------------------------------------
# 4) FORCED TRANSLATIONS FOR ONE LANGUAGE DIRECTION
# -------------------------------------------------------------------
# A section starts with [from>to] (en = English, tr = Turkish).
# Below it:   term in the original language = translation
# Use them for technical terms the translator gets wrong. Avoid everyday
# words with several meanings ("play", "prompt", "session", "dikkat"):
# forcing those would change normal sentences too.
# Turkish words with endings are matched too ("filial terapide").
# Longer terms win over shorter ones ("child-centered play therapy"
# before "play therapy").
